# Seed 67 on the AMD Ryzen 7 PRO 8840HS - islands at 10 hours: 4.5% ahead of seed 64; the load explains half the fast end's optimism (Iteration 458)

`seed 67 10h widths noevolved islands then experiment footprint.py ... then experiment loadtest.py ... then experiment
review-checks.py spread` at e1285e3b1 (Iteration 455): islands the default, the second freeze (alignhot, jithelp, jitre
off). The evolution 17:03:42; it evolved 9 h 15 min. 35,703 designs, generations 0-139: 27,167 alive, 8,536 screened
(24.7%), none died.

**No verdict** - no comparison step. **Islands, the third run, against seed 64 at seed 67's last generation, 139**:
fastest **0.1197 against 0.1253 - 4.5% ahead**; smallest 14,103 against 14,051 (+0.37%); best Q -2.9%; hypervolume -1.7%;
32 bits 2.1% ahead. **The second freeze, against seed 66 at generation 90** (the chain's spread over seeds 63-67):
fastest 0.1235 against 0.1286, smallest 14,135 against 14,077 (+0.4%) - no loss. By its end: fastest 0.120, smallest 14,103.

**The load test** (lab/evolve/loadtest.py, the owner's question of 454): its 8 fastest and 4 slowest against s6, idle and
with three other cores running s6 - **the fast group 0.947 loaded / idle (0.887-0.983), the slow group 0.994** (0.990-
1.012). Under load the fast designs measure about 5% faster against s6 and the slow ones do not: the load accounts for
about half of the fast end's optimism (its fastest tenth: 1.083), the rest the selection's own luck.

**Facts.** Its front: 152 designs, 75 with the JIT; tos on 79. heldout (sieve): Spearman 0.971, the fastest tenth -0.046
(the top's order noise there), the pick's regret +3.0%. No 32-bit failure. I+ on 25 front designs, all with the fuser
(16% of the front). The gene votes over seeds 65-67: candidate **jitret** (12 of 12). The footprint check (its 16
fastest): Spearman 0.212 at 100 words, -0.026 at 10,000 - the 16 too close to rank. Its chain's remote checks, of seed
66's front: results/xcheck-seed67.md.

---
# Evolved VM designs

27167 designs evaluated, 27167 alive, 8536 screened - not measured (Iteration 200). Speed is the geometric mean of median cpu time / hand-made s6, long workloads (Iteration 100) over kernel, fib, corpus, loop, bubble, ackermann,
relative to s6-cv8b; size is the self-hosting image, binary the stripped engine. sieve, parse is held out.
The front is over three objectives, equal (Iteration 75): speed, binary + image, peak memory - memory within 8 KB a tie (Iteration 77).


The Q-best design (binary + image x run time x memory - the knee; information): 5f6f2ba04f, speed 0.122, binary + image 30,960, memory 180 KB - Q 0.207 of s6-cv8b's.

## The front's genomes

Each design above, and any other the front was measured again with, as one JSON line: its record without the raw timings. A tool that takes a database (--db) reads this report as one - the lines that are not JSON are skipped - so results/ is all a new chat needs to rebuild a front design.

The load explains the fast end's optimism if the fast group's median is near 0.90 and the slow group's near 1.00.
